<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the $`\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell`$-linear restricted geometric Langlands equivalence for smooth projective connected curves and connected reductive groups in characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; 0, with <i>ℓ</i> ≠ <i>p</i>, in two regimes. Over $`\overline{\mathbb F}_q`$, we assume the four characteristic conditions of the restricted theory stated below. Over arbitrary algebraically closed fields, we assume these conditions and additionally that <i>p</i> is very good and does not divide the Weyl-group order. This proves Gaitsgory–Raskin's full-support conjecture [[12, Conjecture 1.3.10]](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02237v1) in these regimes.

- **claim-full-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-full-support)): Let $k,\ell,E,X,G$ be as above. Under either Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:A" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:A">2</a> or Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:B" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:B">3</a>, the inclusion $Y'\subset Y$ is equality as spectral prestacks. Consequently the given functor is a $\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y)$-linear equivalence $$\mathbb L_G^{\mathrm{restr}}: \mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(X))\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(Y).$$
  - Explanation: In both characteristic regimes the primed open-and-closed union of spectral components is the whole restricted spectral prestack, so the restricted Langlands functor is a linear equivalence onto all nilpotent ind-coherent sheaves.
- **claim-rational-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-rational-support)): For the rational datum just specified, every nonempty open-and-closed substack $U\subset Y_0$ has $$\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(U)\underset{\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y_0)}\otimes\mathcal C_0\ne0.$$ Consequently, if $Y'_0\subset Y_0$ is an open-and-closed support union whose complementary structure idempotent acts by zero on $\mathcal C_0$, then $Y'_0=Y_0$ as spectral prestacks. In particular this applies to the support of any given $\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y_0)$-linear primed equivalence.
  - Explanation: For the given rational spectral-action datum, no nonempty open-and-closed substack of the rational spectral prestack can act by zero on the rational automorphic category, so any open-and-closed support union is everything.
- **claim-missing-component-object** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-missing)): Suppose a connected component $C\subset Y$ is disjoint from $Y'$. Then $$\mathcal D_C :=\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(C)\underset{\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y)}\otimes\mathcal D_Y$$ is nonzero, and $\Psi_{\mathcal B}(D)=0$ for every $D\in\mathcal D_C$. Moreover, there exists a nonzero $F\in\mathcal D_C$ that is compact both in $\mathcal C_K$ and in $\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}(\mathcal B_K)$. For every finite-type smooth scheme chart $b:U\to\mathcal B$, the complex $b_K^*F$ is bounded constructible.
  - Explanation: A component missing from the primed union produces a nonzero compact generic object whose specialization vanishes and which is constructible on smooth charts.
- **claim-hecke-vanishing-arbitrary-leg** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-vanishing)): Let $a:T\to\mathcal B$ be smooth, where $T$ is a scheme or algebraic space of finite type over $S$, and let $\ell_T:T\to\mathscr X$ be any $S$-morphism. For $F$ as in Proposition <a href="#spec:missing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:missing">6</a> and every finite-dimensional representation $V$ of $\check G$, one has $$\Psi_T(a_K^*F)=0, \qquad \Psi_T\bigl((a,\ell_T)_K^*\mathrm H_V(F)\bigr)=0.$$ The same statements hold on the smooth locus of $a$, and after any of the finite trait replacements described above.
  - Explanation: Nearby cycles of the object and of its Hecke transforms vanish after pullback along any map smooth over the bundle stack, with an arbitrary leg map.
- **claim-levi-reduction-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lie-input)): Let $k$ and $G$ satisfy Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a> and either Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:A" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:A">2</a> or Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:B" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:B">3</a>, and let $\kappa$ be the invariant symmetric form in those hypotheses. Suppose that $a(t)\in\mathfrak g[[t]]$ satisfies $\chi(a(0))\ne\chi(0)$. After a change of frame by an element of $G(k[[t]])$, there are a Levi subgroup $M\subset G$ and a cocharacter $\lambda:\mathbb G_m\to Z(M)$ such that $$\label{eq:lie-output} \begin{gathered} a(t)\in\mathfrak m[[t]],\qquad \kappa(a(0),d\lambda)\ne0,\\ \operatorname{ad}_{a(0)}:\mathfrak g/\mathfrak m\longrightarrow \mathfrak g/\mathfrak m\ \text{is invertible}. \end{gathered}$$ Here $\operatorname{ad}_x(y)=[x,y]$. The subgroup $M$ may equal $G$, and the invertibility assertion includes the zero vector space.
  - Explanation: A formal Higgs field with non-nilpotent constant term can be moved into a Levi subalgebra, with a central cocharacter pairing nontrivially and an invertible adjoint action on the quotient.
- **claim-root-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lie-root-pairing)): The form $\kappa$ is nondegenerate on $\mathfrak t$. For every root $\alpha$, there is a scalar $c_\alpha\in k^\times$ such that $$\label{eq:root-coroot-pairing} \kappa(d\alpha^\vee,h)=c_\alpha\,d\alpha(h) \qquad(h\in\mathfrak t).$$ In particular, neither $d\alpha$ nor $d\alpha^\vee$ vanishes. Every element of $\mathfrak g$ is conjugate into the Lie algebra of a Borel subgroup containing $T$. Every semisimple element is conjugate into $\mathfrak t$.
  - Explanation: The invariant form is nondegenerate on the torus Lie algebra and pairs root differentials with coroot differentials by nonzero scalars, so neither vanishes.
- **claim-constant-term-jordan** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lie-constant-parts)): Suppose that $a_0=s+n\in\mathfrak g$, where $s,n\in\mathfrak g$, $s$ is semisimple, $n$ is nilpotent in a faithful representation, and $[s,n]=0$. Suppose also that $\chi(a_0)\ne\chi(0)$. After conjugation, $s$ is a nonzero element of $\mathfrak t$ and $M=Z_G(s)$ is a Levi subgroup. One has $a_0\in\mathfrak m$ and $\operatorname{ad}_{a_0}$ is invertible on $\mathfrak g/\mathfrak m$. For every $\lambda:\mathbb G_m\to Z(M)$, $$\label{eq:nilpotent-orthogonality} \kappa(a_0,d\lambda)=\kappa(s,d\lambda).$$
  - Explanation: For a non-nilpotent constant term with commuting Jordan parts, the semisimple part is nonzero, its centralizer is a Levi, the adjoint action is invertible off it, and the nilpotent part does not affect the pairing with central cocharacters.
- **claim-central-lattice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lie-lattice)): Assume the invariant-form and Levi-center conditions of Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a>. Every Levi Cartan determinant is prime to $p$. For every Levi subgroup $M$ containing $T$, $$\label{eq:central-span} \operatorname{span}_k\{d\lambda: \lambda\in X_*(Z(M))\}=\mathfrak z(\mathfrak m).$$ Here $X_*(Z(M))$ denotes actual cocharacters of the group center, viewed in $X_*(T)$.
  - Explanation: Differentials of genuine central cocharacters span the Lie-algebra center of each Levi, and Levi Cartan determinants are prime to the characteristic.
- **claim-formal-levi-conjugation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lie-formal)): Let $G$ be a connected reductive group over an algebraically closed field $k$, let $M\subset G$ be a Levi subgroup, and let $a(t)\in\mathfrak g[[t]]$ satisfy $a_0=a(0)\in\mathfrak m$. If $\operatorname{ad}_{a_0}$ is invertible on $\mathfrak g/\mathfrak m$, then there exists $g(t)\in G(k[[t]])$ with $g(0)=1$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(g(t))a(t)\in\mathfrak m[[t]]$.
  - Explanation: Successive formal conjugations, using invertibility of the adjoint action on the quotient, place the whole arc in the Levi subalgebra without exponentials.
- **claim-hecke-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hecke-models)): Both $\widetilde p$ and $q$ are representable, separated, and smooth of relative dimension $m$. The correspondence $H$ is open in a bounded correspondence $\overline H$ for which both projections to $\mathcal Z$ are representable and proper. On the output side the bound has dominant label $\mu^\vee$.* *For the irreducible dual-group representation $V$ corresponding to this bound, with the input and output convention just specified, the generic Hecke functor has the form $$\label{hecke:kernel} \mathrm H_V(F)=\overline q_{K,*}\mathcal I_K.$$ On $H_K$, the integrand $\mathcal I_K$ is $p_K^*F$ tensored with a fixed invertible constant complex. On a fixed-leg affine-Grassmannian fiber, the perverse intersection-cohomology normalization of that complex is $E[m]$, with the constant Tate line if weight normalization is used.
  - Explanation: The Hecke correspondence maps to the product of bundle stack and curve are smooth of the same relative dimension, sit openly in a proper bounded correspondence, and the generic Hecke functor is a pushforward of a twisted pullback.
- **claim-transverse-modification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hecke-transverse)): Assume the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#lie:input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lie:input">8</a>. Let $P\in\mathcal B_s(k)$ and let $A\in T_P^*\mathcal B_s$ be non-nilpotent. There exist a point $x\in X(k)$, a cocharacter $\lambda$, a modification $h\in H^\lambda_s(k)$ from $P$ to a bundle $P'$, and a covector $$\theta=(A',\xi)\in T^*_{(P',x)}(\mathcal B_s\times X), \qquad \xi\ne0,$$ such that $A'$ agrees with $A$ away from $x$ and $$\label{hecke:cotangent-identity} dp_h^*A=dq_h^*\theta.$$ Moreover, form the fixed-side fibers $$H^{\mathrm{in}}_{P,x}=\widetilde p^{-1}(P,x), \qquad H^{\mathrm{out}}_{P',x}=q^{-1}(P',x),$$ with the fixed-bundle identifications retained. On the first fiber, restriction of $p^*A$ to the $q$-relative tangent bundle defines a section $S_A$ of $\Omega_{H_s/\mathcal Z_s,q}$. On the second fiber, restriction of $o^*A'$ to the $\widetilde p$-relative tangent bundle similarly defines $S_{A'}$. Both sections vanish at $h$, and both derivatives $$\begin{aligned} (dS_A)_h &:T_hH^{\mathrm{in}}_{P,x} \longrightarrow (T_hH^{\mathrm{out}}_{P',x})^*,\\ (dS_{A'})_h &:T_hH^{\mathrm{out}}_{P',x} \longrightarrow (T_hH^{\mathrm{in}}_{P,x})^* \end{aligned}$$ are isomorphisms. In particular, each section has an isolated reduced zero at $h$.
  - Explanation: A non-nilpotent Higgs field admits a modification with a nonzero leg covector such that both relative sections have invertible derivatives, hence isolated reduced zeros.
- **claim-quadratic-vertex-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-two-vertex)): Let $F\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}(\mathcal B_K)$ be the object of Proposition <a href="#spec:missing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:missing">6</a>, with the vanishings of Lemma <a href="#spec:vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:vanishing">7</a>. Let $b:U\to\mathcal B$ be a smooth chart, where $U$ is a scheme of finite type over $S$. Let $f\in\mathcal O(U)$ and $u\in U_s(k)$ satisfy $f(u)=0$. Suppose $$df_u=b^*A \quad\text{for a non-nilpotent }A\in T^*_{b(u)}\mathcal B_s.$$ Then there exist $x\in\mathscr X_s(k)$ and an integer $m\geq0$ with the following property. Put $T=U\times_S\mathscr X$, let $F_T$ be the pullback of $F_U=b_K^*F$, and set $$Q^a=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^m u_i v_i+f=0\right\} \subset T\times_S\mathbb A_S^{2m}, \qquad \tau:Q^a\longrightarrow T.$$ Here $u_i,v_i$ are affine coordinates, distinct from the point $u$, and $f$ is pulled back from $U$. At the point $\nu=(u,x,0,0)$ one has $$\label{eq:two-vertex} \bigl(\Psi_{Q^a}(\tau_K^*F_T)\bigr)_\nu=0.$$ When $m=0$, this is the vanishing on $Q^a=V(f)\times_S\mathscr X$.
  - Explanation: Using two Hecke correspondences, nearby cycles vanish at the vertex of an exact local quadratic model attached to a non-nilpotent covector.
- **claim-quadric-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-slice-quadric)): Let $K_0$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\ell$, let $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$, and let $B$ be a finite-type $K_0$-scheme. For $f\in\Gamma(B,\mathcal O_B)$ put $i:D=V(f)\hookrightarrow B$. If $m\geq1$, consider $$g:Q_f=\left\{\sum_{a=1}^{m}u_av_a+fw^2=0\right\} \subset\mathbb P_B^{2m}\longrightarrow B.$$ The powers of the hyperplane class define a distinguished triangle $$\label{eq:slice-quadric-triangle} \bigoplus_{j=0}^{2m-1}E_B(-j)[-2j] \longrightarrow Rg_*E_{Q_f} \longrightarrow i_*E_D(-m)[-2m] \longrightarrow \left(\bigoplus_{j=0}^{2m-1}E_B(-j)[-2j]\right)[1].$$ The identification of its third term with the displayed Tate line requires only a choice of generator of a fixed one-dimensional vector space. Neither $B$ nor $D$ is required to be smooth or reduced.
  - Explanation: Hyperplane powers on a projective quadric family give a triangle whose third term is a shifted twisted constant sheaf on the hypersurface, with no smoothness or reducedness needed.
- **claim-hypersurface-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-slice-hypersurface)): Let $b:U\to\mathcal B$ be smooth, with $U$ a finite-type $S$-scheme. Let $f$ be a regular function on $U$, let $D=V(f)$, and let $u\in D_s(k)$. If $$d(f_s)_u\notin(\mathcal N_U)_u,$$ then $$\bigl(\Psi_D(F_U\|_{D_K})\bigr)_u=0.$$
  - Explanation: If the differential of the defining function avoids the pulled-back nilpotent cone, nearby cycles of the object vanish on the hypersurface at that point.
- **claim-incidence-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-slice-incidence)): Let $B$ be a finite-type scheme over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\ell$. Let $f_1,\ldots,f_d$ be regular functions on $B$, where $d\geq1$, and set $i:J=V(f_1,\ldots,f_d)\hookrightarrow B$. For $$r:I=\left\{\sum_{a=1}^d c_af_a=0\right\} \subset B\times\mathbb P^{d-1}\longrightarrow B,$$ the hyperplane powers give a distinguished triangle $$\label{eq:slice-incidence-triangle} \bigoplus_{j=0}^{d-2}E_B(-j)[-2j] \longrightarrow Rr_*E_I \longrightarrow i_*E_J(-(d-1))[-2(d-1)] \longrightarrow \left(\bigoplus_{j=0}^{d-2}E_B(-j)[-2j]\right)[1].$$ For $d=1$ the first term is zero and $I=J$.
  - Explanation: For several functions, hyperplane powers on a projective incidence family give a triangle isolating a shifted twisted constant sheaf on the common zero locus.
- **claim-higher-codimension-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-slice-higher)): Let $b:U\to\mathcal B$ be smooth, with $U$ a finite-type $S$-scheme, and let $F_U=b_K^*F$ as above. Suppose that $u\in U_s(k)$ and $f_1,\ldots,f_d$ are regular functions vanishing at $u$. Assume that their special-fiber differentials at $u$ are linearly independent and that $$\operatorname{span}_k\{d(f_{1,s})_u,\ldots,d(f_{d,s})_u\} \cap(\mathcal N_U)_u=\{0\}.$$ Then, for $J=V(f_1,\ldots,f_d)$, $$(\Psi_J(F_U\|_{J_K}))_u=0.$$ The assertion includes $d=0$, with $J=U$.
  - Explanation: Vanishing of nearby cycles extends to slices cut by several equations whose independent differentials span a plane meeting the nilpotent cone only at zero.
- **claim-bundle-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-support-extension)): Let $\mathscr X\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be the smooth proper curve fixed in Section <a href="#sec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:specialization">2</a>, and let $K$ be the fixed algebraic closure of $\operatorname{Frac}(R)$. Every $G$-bundle on $\mathscr X_K$ extends to a $G$-bundle on $\mathscr X_{R'}$ for some finite extension $\operatorname{Frac}(R')/\operatorname{Frac}(R)$ inside $K$, where $R'$ is the integral closure of $R$ in that extension.
  - Explanation: Every bundle on the geometric generic fiber extends over the integral closure after a finite extension, via a chain of bounded one-point modifications from the trivial bundle.
- **claim-finite-slice-nonzero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-support-finite-slice)): Let $R$ be a complete discrete valuation ring with algebraically closed residue field $k$ of characteristic different from $\ell$, let $S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$, and let $K$ be an algebraic closure of its fraction field. Let $U/S$ be a smooth affine scheme of constant relative dimension $N$, let $\mathcal F$ be a bounded constructible $E$-complex on $U_K$, and let $\Lambda\subset T^*U_s$ be a closed conical subset with $\dim\Lambda\leq N$. Suppose that the closure in $U$ of the nonzero-stalk locus of $\mathcal F$ meets $U_s$. Then, after a finite extension of the trait and shrinking $U$, there exist $u\in U_s(k)$, an integer $0\leq d\leq N$, and functions $f_1,\ldots,f_d$ vanishing at $u$ such that their special-fiber differentials are independent, $$\operatorname{span}\{d(f_{1,s})_u,\ldots,d(f_{d,s})_u\} \cap\Lambda_u\subseteq\{0\},$$ and, for $J=V(f_1,\ldots,f_d)$, $$\label{eq:support-nonzero} \bigl(\Psi_J(\mathcal F\|_{J_K})\bigr)_u\ne0.$$ For $d=0$, the list of functions is empty and $J=U$.
  - Explanation: If the support closure of a constructible complex meets the special fiber, a transverse slice exists on which nearby cycles are nonzero, because they reduce to a finite sum of stalks.
- **claim-component-categories** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-components)): Under either Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:A" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:A">2</a> or Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:B" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:B">3</a>, every connected component $Z\subset Y$ has a nonzero localized automorphic category. The restriction of the given spectral-linear Langlands functor induces an equivalence $$\mathcal C_{s,Z}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(Z).$$
  - Explanation: Each connected component of the spectral prestack has a nonzero localized automorphic category equivalent to nilpotent ind-coherent sheaves on it.
- **claim-arithmetic-formula** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-arithmetic)): For the finite-field datum of Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:A" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:A">2</a>, there is a canonical isomorphism $$\mathop{\mathrm{Funct}}_c\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G_0}(\mathbb F_q),E\bigr) \simeq \Gamma^{\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}}\bigl(Y^{\mathrm{arithm}}, \omega_{Y^{\mathrm{arithm}}}\bigr).$$ The left side is the vector space of compactly supported functions on isomorphism classes of rational bundles, regarded as a complex concentrated in degree zero. The right side uses the ind-coherent dualizing object and ind-coherent global sections on the derived fixed-point stack.
  - Explanation: Compactly supported functions on rational bundles are identified with ind-coherent global sections of the dualizing object on the derived Frobenius fixed-point stack.
- **claim-parameter-adjoint** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-adjoint)): Let $\mathcal A_0$ be a compactly generated, presentable, stable, $E$-linear symmetric monoidal category whose compact objects are dualizable. Assume its tensor product preserves colimits separately. Let $a:\mathcal A_0\to\mathop{\mathrm{Vect}}_E$ be a continuous $E$-linear symmetric monoidal functor, and give $\mathop{\mathrm{Vect}}_E$ its $\mathcal A_0$-action through $a$. The right adjoint $R_a$ of $a$ is continuous and carries a coherent $\mathcal A_0$-linear structure. Moreover, $R_a(E)\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The right adjoint of a continuous symmetric monoidal functor to vector spaces is continuous, carries a coherent linear structure, and does not kill the ground field.
- **claim-hecke-eigenobjects** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-eigenobjects)): Under either Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:A" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:A">2</a> or Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:B" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:B">3</a>, every parameter $\sigma:\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(E)\to Y$ admits a nonzero object $M_\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G)$ with a coherent Hecke eigenstructure of eigenvalue $\sigma$. If $\sigma$ belongs to a component $Z$, then $M_\sigma\in\mathcal C_{s,Z}$. The assertion concerns the ind-constructible dg category and includes no boundedness, perversity or Weil structure.
  - Explanation: Every parameter admits a nonzero automorphic object with a coherent Hecke eigenstructure, lying in the localized category of the component of the parameter.
- **claim-coefficient-sheaves** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coeff-sheaves)): Extension of coefficients induces equivalences $$\begin{aligned} \mathop{\mathrm{Vect}}_E\otimes_{E_0}\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{E_0}(T)&\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_E(T), \label{eq:coeff-ambient}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{Vect}}_E\otimes_{E_0}\mathop{\mathrm{QLisse}}_{E_0}(X)&\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{QLisse}}_E(X), \label{eq:coeff-qlisse}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{Vect}}_E\otimes_{E_0}\mathcal C_0&\simeq\mathcal C_E. \label{eq:coeff-nilp} \end{aligned}$$ The first equivalence also holds on locally finite-type algebraic stacks by smooth descent. The second is symmetric monoidal and respects the t-structures used in the tensor-functor definition of $Y_0$ and $Y$. On smooth charts, the third respects the cohomological nilpotent condition. The free and forgetful functors in these comparisons are t-exact for the ordinary and perverse t-structures whenever these are used.
  - Explanation: Scalar extension of coefficients is an equivalence on ind-constructible sheaves, on quasi-lisse sheaves and on the nilpotent automorphic categories.
- **claim-coefficient-spectral** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coeff-spectral)): There are natural identifications $$\label{eq:coeff-spectral} Y_0\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}E_0}\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}E\simeq Y, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Vect}}_E\otimes_{E_0}\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y_0)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y).$$ The second is symmetric monoidal. These identifications compare the universal evaluation systems for every finite set of legs.
  - Explanation: The rational spectral prestack base-changes to the geometric one and quasi-coherent sheaves compare monoidally, including the universal evaluation systems.
- **claim-coefficient-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coeff-action)): Suppose $X\ne\mathbb P^1$ and equip $\mathcal C_0$ with the rational spectral-action datum of Theorem <a href="#thm:rational-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rational-support">5</a>. Under Lemmas <a href="#lem:coeff-sheaves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:coeff-sheaves">29</a> and <a href="#lem:coeff-spectral" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:coeff-spectral">30</a>, its scalar extension is the established $\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y)$-action on $\mathcal C_E$.
  - Explanation: Away from the projective line, scalar extension of the given rational spectral action is the established geometric action over the algebraically closed coefficient field.
- **claim-clopen-support-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coeff-idempotent)): Suppose the coefficient comparisons <a href="#eq:coeff-nilp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coeff-nilp">[eq:coeff-nilp]</a> and <a href="#eq:coeff-spectral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coeff-spectral">[eq:coeff-spectral]</a> identify the two spectral actions. If an open-and-closed substack $U\subset Y_0$ acts by zero on $\mathcal C_0$, then $U=\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: An open-and-closed substack acting by zero on the rational automorphic category must be empty, by comparing actions and descending along faithful flatness.
- **claim-rational-unpriming** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-rational-arithmetic)): Let $Y'_0\subset Y_0$ be a Frobenius-stable open-and-closed support union as in Theorem <a href="#thm:rational-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rational-support">5</a>. In addition to the rational hypotheses, assume a canonical primed trace isomorphism over $E_0$ is given: $$\mathop{\mathrm{Funct}}_c\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G_0}(\mathbb F_q),E_0\bigr) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \Gamma^{\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}}\bigl((Y'_0)^{\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}}, \omega_{(Y'_0)^{\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}}}\bigr).$$ Assume the given map intertwines the excursion and spherical Hecke actions in the same Frobenius and Satake conventions. Then the same canonical formula and compatibilities hold with $Y_0^{\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}}$ in place of $(Y'_0)^{\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}}$.
  - Explanation: Given a canonical primed trace isomorphism over the rational coefficient field with compatible Hecke actions, the same formula holds with the full Frobenius fixed-point prestack.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lie-constant-parts",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $a_0=s+n\\in\\mathfrak g$, where $s,n\\in\\mathfrak g$, $s$ is semisimple, $n$ is nilpotent in a faithful representation, and $[s,n]=0$. One has $a_0\\in\\mathfrak m$ and $\\operatorname{ad}_{a_0}$ is invertible on $\\mathfrak g/\\mathfrak m$. For every $\\lambda:\\mathbb G_m\\to Z(M)$, $$\\label{eq:nilpotent-orthogonality}\n \\kappa(a_0,d\\lambda)=\\kappa(s,d\\lambda).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-014.5"

        "exact_quote": "Both $\\widetilde p$ and $q$ are representable, separated, and smooth of relative dimension $m$. On the output side the bound has dominant label $\\mu^\\vee$.*\n\n*For the irreducible dual-group representation $V$ corresponding to this bound, with the input and output convention just specified, the generic Hecke functor has the form $$\\label{hecke:kernel}\n \\mathrm H_V(F)=\\overline q_{K,*}\\mathcal I_K.$$ On $H_K$, the integrand $\\mathcal I_K$ is $p_K^*F$ tensored with a fixed invertible constant complex.         "anchor": "stmt-hecke-transverse",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#lie:input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lie:input\">8</a>. Let $P\\in\\mathcal B_s(k)$ and let $A\\in T_P^*\\mathcal B_s$ be non-nilpotent. There exist a point $x\\in X(k)$, a cocharacter $\\lambda$, a modification $h\\in H^\\lambda_s(k)$ from $P$ to a bundle $P'$, and a covector $$\\theta=(A',\\xi)\\in T^*_{(P',x)}(\\mathcal B_s\\times X),\n \\qquad \\xi\\ne0,$$ such that $A'$ agrees with $A$ away from $x$ and $$\\label{hecke:cotangent-identity}\n dp_h^*A=dq_h^*\\theta.$$ Moreover, form the fixed-side fibers $$H^{\\mathrm{in}}_{P,x}=\\widetilde p^{-1}(P,x),\n \\qquad H^{\\mathrm{out}}_{P',x}=q^{-1}(P',x),$$ with the fixed-bundle identifications retained. On the first fiber, restriction of $p^*A$ to the $q$-relative tangent bundle defines a section $S_A$ of $\\Omega_{H_s/\\mathcal Z_s,q}$. On the second fiber, restriction of $o^*A'$ to the $\\widetilde p$-relative tangent bundle similarly defines $S_{A'}$. Both sections vanish at $h$, and both derivatives $$\\begin{aligned}\n (dS_A)_h &:T_hH^{\\mathrm{in}}_{P,x}\n                  \\longrightarrow (T_hH^{\\mathrm{out}}_{P',x})^*,\\\\\n (dS_{A'})_h &:T_hH^{\\mathrm{out}}_{P',x}\n                  \\longrightarrow (T_hH^{\\mathrm{in}}_{P,x})^*\n\\end{aligned}$$ are isomorphisms.       "exact_statement": "Assume the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#lie:input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lie:input\">8</a>. Let $P\\in\\mathcal B_s(k)$ and let $A\\in T_P^*\\mathcal B_s$ be non-nilpotent. There exist a point $x\\in X(k)$, a cocharacter $\\lambda$, a modification $h\\in H^\\lambda_s(k)$ from $P$ to a bundle $P'$, and a covector $$\\theta=(A',\\xi)\\in T^*_{(P',x)}(\\mathcal B_s\\times X),\n \\qquad \\xi\\ne0,$$ such that $A'$ agrees with $A$ away from $x$ and $$\\label{hecke:cotangent-identity}\n dp_h^*A=dq_h^*\\theta.$$ Moreover, form the fixed-side fibers $$H^{\\mathrm{in}}_{P,x}=\\widetilde p^{-1}(P,x),\n \\qquad H^{\\mathrm{out}}_{P',x}=q^{-1}(P',x),$$ with the fixed-bundle identifications retained. On the first fiber, restriction of $p^*A$ to the $q$-relative tangent bundle defines a section $S_A$ of $\\Omega_{H_s/\\mathcal Z_s,q}$. On the second fiber, restriction of $o^*A'$ to the $\\widetilde p$-relative tangent bundle similarly defines $S_{A'}$. Both sections vanish at $h$, and both derivatives $$\\begin{aligned}\n (dS_A)_h &:T_hH^{\\mathrm{in}}_{P,x}\n                  \\longrightarrow (T_hH^{\\mathrm{out}}_{P',x})^*,\\\\\n (dS_{A'})_h &:T_hH^{\\mathrm{out}}_{P',x}\n                  \\longrightarrow (T_hH^{\\mathrm{in}}_{P,x})^*\n\\end{aligned}$$ are isomorphisms. In particular, each section has an isolated reduced zero at $h$.",
      "explanation": "A non-nilpotent Higgs field admits a modification with a nonzero leg covector such that both relative sections have invertible derivatives, hence isolated reduced zeros.",

        "anchor": "stmt-two-vertex",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Shv}}(\\mathcal B_K)$ be the object of Proposition <a href=\"#spec:missing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:missing\">6</a>, with the vanishings of Lemma <a href=\"#spec:vanishing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:vanishing\">7</a>. Let $b:U\\to\\mathcal B$ be a smooth chart, where $U$ is a scheme of finite type over $S$. Let $f\\in\\mathcal O(U)$ and $u\\in U_s(k)$ satisfy $f(u)=0$. Suppose $$df_u=b^*A\n \\quad\\text{for a non-nilpotent }A\\in T^*_{b(u)}\\mathcal B_s.$$ Then there exist $x\\in\\mathscr X_s(k)$ and an integer $m\\geq0$ with the following property. Put $T=U\\times_S\\mathscr X$, let $F_T$ be the pullback of $F_U=b_K^*F$, and set $$Q^a=\\left\\{\\sum_{i=1}^m u_i v_i+f=0\\right\\}\n       \\subset T\\times_S\\mathbb A_S^{2m},\n \\qquad \\tau:Q^a\\longrightarrow T.$$ Here $u_i,v_i$ are affine coordinates, distinct from the point $u$, and $f$ is pulled back from $U$. At the point $\\nu=(u,x,0,0)$ one has $$\\label{eq:two-vertex}\n \\bigl(\\Psi_{Q^a}(\\tau_K^*F_T)\\bigr)_\\nu=0.$$ When $m=0$, this is the vanishing on $Q^a=V(f)\\times_S\\mathscr X$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-slice-quadric",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K_0$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\\ell$, let $E=\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_{\\ell}$, and let $B$ be a finite-type $K_0$-scheme. For $f\\in\\Gamma(B,\\mathcal O_B)$ put $i:D=V(f)\\hookrightarrow B$. If $m\\geq1$, consider $$g:Q_f=\\left\\{\\sum_{a=1}^{m}u_av_a+fw^2=0\\right\\}\n       \\subset\\mathbb P_B^{2m}\\longrightarrow B.$$ The powers of the hyperplane class define a distinguished triangle $$\\label{eq:slice-quadric-triangle}\n \\bigoplus_{j=0}^{2m-1}E_B(-j)[-2j]\n \\longrightarrow Rg_*E_{Q_f}\n \\longrightarrow i_*E_D(-m)[-2m]\n \\longrightarrow\n \\left(\\bigoplus_{j=0}^{2m-1}E_B(-j)[-2j]\\right)[1].$$ The identification of its third term with the displayed Tate line requires only a choice of generator of a fixed one-dimensional vector space. Neither $B$ nor $D$ is required to be smooth or reduced.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.5"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a finite-type scheme over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\\ell$. For $$r:I=\\left\\{\\sum_{a=1}^d c_af_a=0\\right\\}\n           \\subset B\\times\\mathbb P^{d-1}\\longrightarrow B,$$ the hyperplane powers give a distinguished triangle $$\\label{eq:slice-incidence-triangle}\n \\bigoplus_{j=0}^{d-2}E_B(-j)[-2j]\n \\longrightarrow Rr_*E_I\n \\longrightarrow i_*E_J(-(d-1))[-2(d-1)]\n \\longrightarrow\n \\left(\\bigoplus_{j=0}^{d-2}E_B(-j)[-2j]\\right)[1].$$ For $d=1$ the first term is zero and $I=J$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $b:U\\to\\mathcal B$ be smooth, with $U$ a finite-type $S$-scheme, and let $F_U=b_K^*F$ as above.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-support-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr X\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be the smooth proper curve fixed in Section <a href=\"#sec:specialization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:specialization\">2</a>, and let $K$ be the fixed algebraic closure of $\\operatorname{Frac}(R)$. Every $G$-bundle on $\\mathscr X_K$ extends to a $G$-bundle on $\\mathscr X_{R'}$ for some finite extension $\\operatorname{Frac}(R')/\\operatorname{Frac}(R)$ inside $K$, where $R'$ is the integral closure of $R$ in that extension.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-support-extension",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr X\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be the smooth proper curve fixed in Section <a href=\"#sec:specialization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:specialization\">2</a>, and let $K$ be the fixed algebraic closure of $\\operatorname{Frac}(R)$. Every $G$-bundle on $\\mathscr X_K$ extends to a $G$-bundle on $\\mathscr X_{R'}$ for some finite extension $\\operatorname{Frac}(R')/\\operatorname{Frac}(R)$ inside $K$, where $R'$ is the integral closure of $R$ in that extension.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathscr X\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be the smooth proper curve fixed in Section <a href=\"#sec:specialization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:specialization\">2</a>, and let $K$ be the fixed algebraic closure of $\\operatorname{Frac}(R)$. Every $G$-bundle on $\\mathscr X_K$ extends to a $G$-bundle on $\\mathscr X_{R'}$ for some finite extension $\\operatorname{Frac}(R')/\\operatorname{Frac}(R)$ inside $K$, where $R'$ is the integral closure of $R$ in that extension.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-support-finite-slice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a complete discrete valuation ring with algebraically closed residue field $k$ of characteristic different from $\\ell$, let $S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$, and let $K$ be an algebraic closure of its fraction field. Let $U/S$ be a smooth affine scheme of constant relative dimension $N$, let $\\mathcal F$ be a bounded constructible $E$-complex on $U_K$, and let $\\Lambda\\subset T^*U_s$ be a closed conical subset with $\\dim\\Lambda\\leq N$. Suppose that the closure in $U$ of the nonzero-stalk locus of $\\mathcal F$ meets $U_s$. Then, after a finite extension of the trait and shrinking $U$, there exist $u\\in U_s(k)$, an integer $0\\leq d\\leq N$, and functions $f_1,\\ldots,f_d$ vanishing at $u$ such that their special-fiber differentials are independent, $$\\operatorname{span}\\{d(f_{1,s})_u,\\ldots,d(f_{d,s})_u\\}\n       \\cap\\Lambda_u\\subseteq\\{0\\},$$ and, for $J=V(f_1,\\ldots,f_d)$, $$\\label{eq:support-nonzero}\n \\bigl(\\Psi_J(\\mathcal F|_{J_K})\\bigr)_u\\ne0.$$ For $d=0$, the list of functions is empty and $J=U$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-support-finite-slice",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a complete discrete valuation ring with algebraically closed residue field $k$ of characteristic different from $\\ell$, let $S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$, and let $K$ be an algebraic closure of its fraction field. Let $U/S$ be a smooth affine scheme of constant relative dimension $N$, let $\\mathcal F$ be a bounded constructible $E$-complex on $U_K$, and let $\\Lambda\\subset T^*U_s$ be a closed conical subset with $\\dim\\Lambda\\leq N$. Suppose that the closure in $U$ of the nonzero-stalk locus of $\\mathcal F$ meets $U_s$. Then, after a finite extension of the trait and shrinking $U$, there exist $u\\in U_s(k)$, an integer $0\\leq d\\leq N$, and functions $f_1,\\ldots,f_d$ vanishing at $u$ such that their special-fiber differentials are independent, $$\\operatorname{span}\\{d(f_{1,s})_u,\\ldots,d(f_{d,s})_u\\}\n       \\cap\\Lambda_u\\subseteq\\{0\\},$$ and, for $J=V(f_1,\\ldots,f_d)$, $$\\label{eq:support-nonzero}\n \\bigl(\\Psi_J(\\mathcal F|_{J_K})\\bigr)_u\\ne0.$$ For $d=0$, the list of functions is empty and $J=U$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $R$ be a complete discrete valuation ring with algebraically closed residue field $k$ of characteristic different from $\\ell$, let $S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$, and let $K$ be an algebraic closure of its fraction field. Suppose that the closure in $U$ of the nonzero-stalk locus of $\\mathcal F$ meets $U_s$. Then, after a finite extension of the trait and shrinking $U$, there exist $u\\in U_s(k)$, an integer $0\\leq d\\leq N$, and functions $f_1,\\ldots,f_d$ vanishing at $u$ such that their special-fiber differentials are independent, $$\\operatorname{span}\\{d(f_{1,s})_u,\\ldots,d(f_{d,s})_u\\}\n       \\cap\\Lambda_u\\subseteq\\{0\\},$$ and, for $J=V(f_1,\\ldots,f_d)$, $$\\label{eq:support-nonzero}\n \\bigl(\\Psi_J(\\mathcal F|_{J_K})\\bigr)_u\\ne0.$$ For $d=0$, the list of functions is empty and $J=U$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A_0$ be a compactly generated, presentable, stable, $E$-linear symmetric monoidal category whose compact objects are dualizable.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-coeff-sheaves",
        "exact_quote": "Extension of coefficients induces equivalences $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Vect}}_E\\otimes_{E_0}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Shv}}_{E_0}(T)&\\simeq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Shv}}_E(T),\n \\label{eq:coeff-ambient}\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Vect}}_E\\otimes_{E_0}\\mathop{\\mathrm{QLisse}}_{E_0}(X)&\\simeq\\mathop{\\mathrm{QLisse}}_E(X),\n \\label{eq:coeff-qlisse}\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Vect}}_E\\otimes_{E_0}\\mathcal C_0&\\simeq\\mathcal C_E.\n \\label{eq:coeff-nilp}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The first equivalence also holds on locally finite-type algebraic stacks by smooth descent. The second is symmetric monoidal and respects the t-structures used in the tensor-functor definition of $Y_0$ and $Y$. On smooth charts, the third respects the cohomological nilpotent condition. The free and forgetful functors in these comparisons are t-exact for the ordinary and perverse t-structures whenever these are used.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-rational-arithmetic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y'_0\\subset Y_0$ be a Frobenius-stable open-and-closed support union as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:rational-support\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:rational-support\">5</a>. In addition to the rational hypotheses, assume a canonical primed trace isomorphism over $E_0$ is given: $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Funct}}_c\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bun}}_{G_0}(\\mathbb F_q),E_0\\bigr)\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n \\Gamma^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{IndCoh}}}\\bigl((Y'_0)^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Frob}}},\n                              \\omega_{(Y'_0)^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Frob}}}}\\bigr).$$ Assume the given map intertwines the excursion and spherical Hecke actions in the same Frobenius and Satake conventions. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Hypothesis common** ([source](#stmt-hyp-common)): The following conditions hold for $G/k$.
- **Regime B base field** ([source](#stmt-hyp-b)): The field $k$ is any algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$.
- **very good prime** ([source](#pm-h-3)): $$ Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a> holds. No additional restriction on the order of the Weyl group is imposed in this regime. </div> <div class="hypothesis"> **Hypothesis 3** (Regime B: arbitrary algebraically closed base). The field $k$ is any algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$. Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a> holds, $p$ is very good for $G$, and $p\nmid \|W_G\|$. No finite-field descent of $X$ is assumed. </div> Here very good has its usual root-system meaning: for type $A_n$, $p\nmid n+1$; for $B_n,C_n,D_n$, $p\ne2$; for $G_2,F_4,E_6,E_7$, $p\ne2,3$; and for $E_8$, $p\ne2,3,5$, on every irreducible factor.
- **Levi subgroup** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A Levi subgroup always means a Levi factor of a parabolic subgroup.
- **parameter** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A parameter is an $E$-point of $Y$, hence such a tensor functor with $T=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}E$.
- **cocharacter differential and adjoint quotient** ([source](#sec:lie)): For a cocharacter $\lambda:\mathbb G_m\to G$, write $d\lambda\in\mathfrak g$ for the image of $1\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathbb G_m)=k$.
- **adjoint quotient** ([source](#sec:lie)): Write $\chi:\mathfrak g\to\mathfrak g/\!/G$ for the adjoint quotient.
- **one-point Hecke modification** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A one-point Hecke modification is a quadruple $(P,P',x,\alpha)$, where $x$ is a point of $\mathscr X$ and $\alpha$ identifies $P'$ with $P$ away from its graph.
- **Hecke eigenobject with coherent structure** ([source](#stmt-def-eigenstructure)): A Hecke eigenobject of eigenvalue $\sigma$ with coherent structure is an object $M\in\mathcal C_s$ equipped, for all finite sets $I$, with isomorphisms $$H_I(V,M)\simeq M\boxtimes\sigma^I(V)$$ natural in $V\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}(\check G)^{\otimes I}$ and compatible with units, tensor products, permutations of the legs and collisions of legs, with all compatibilities imposed homotopy-coherently (Arinkin et al. 2022b, sec. 15.2) [[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)].

| q-riemann-hurwitz-cover | Euler characteristic relation of a finite etale cover of the projective line | = | $-2n$ | covers of degree n |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-missing-component** [claim](#stmt-spec-missing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-missing): Project compact generators of the specialization-linked summand onto the missing component and use that the primed equivalence is spectral-linear. -> nonzero compact object with vanishing specialization
- **step-hecke-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-spec-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-vanishing): Smooth base change plus the tensor formula for lisse sheaves, extended by continuity to the colimit closure containing Hecke transforms. -> vanishing of nearby cycles for smooth charts and Hecke transforms with arbitrary leg
- **step-root-pairing** [claim](#stmt-lie-root-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lie-root-pairing): Weight decomposition of the invariant form, root-coroot bracket identity, and incidence argument for conjugation into a Borel. -> nondegeneracy on the torus and nonvanishing root differentials
- **step-jordan-constant-term** [claim](#stmt-lie-constant-parts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lie-constant-parts): Conjugate the semisimple part into the torus, use restricted nilpotence of the toral projection, and a commuting nilpotent plus invertible semisimple operator. -> Levi centralizer, invertibility on the quotient and orthogonality of the nilpotent part
- **step-central-lattice** [claim](#stmt-lie-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lie-lattice): Cartan matrix rank argument, Cauchy–Binet contradiction for a type A Levi, and an integral projection operator onto central cocharacters. -> central cocharacter differentials span the Lie center
- **step-formal-conjugation** [claim](#stmt-lie-formal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lie-formal): Order-by-order elimination of off-Levi coefficients by conjugation, with t-adic convergence. -> arc conjugated into the Levi
- **step-levi-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lie-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lie-formal-2): Combine the regime-specific construction of Jordan parts and central cocharacter with the formal conjugation. -> Levi and central cocharacter with nonzero pairing
- **step-hecke-models** [claim](#stmt-hecke-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hecke-models): Integral affine Grassmannian models, Beauville–Laszlo gluing, and the involution swapping input and output. -> smooth representable projections and proper bounded correspondence
- **step-transverse-modification** [claim](#stmt-hecke-transverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hecke-transverse): Modify by the central cocharacter, compute the moving-leg covector, and show the residue pairing makes both relative derivatives isomorphisms. -> transverse modification with isolated reduced zeros
- **step-quadratic-vertex** [claim](#stmt-two-vertex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-two-vertex): Two Hecke correspondences, proper base change to isolate the stalk, and an étale chart lift producing the exact quadratic equation. -> vanishing at the vertex of the quadratic model
- **step-quadric-comparison** [claim](#stmt-slice-quadric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-slice-quadric): Cohomology of split quadrics by affine cells, nonvanishing of hyperplane powers, and localization. -> comparison triangle on a quadric family
- **step-hypersurface-test** [claim](#stmt-slice-hypersurface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-slice-hypersurface): Smooth case by the vanishing lemma; otherwise compactify the quadratic model and tensor the quadric triangle with the object. -> vanishing on hypersurfaces whose differential avoids the nilpotent cone
- **step-incidence-comparison** [claim](#stmt-slice-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-slice-incidence): Projective bundle off the common zero locus and localization. -> incidence comparison triangle
- **step-higher-codimension** [claim](#stmt-slice-higher) / [proof](#proof-stmt-slice-higher): Apply the hypersurface test on each chart of the incidence family, then tensor the incidence triangle with the object. -> vanishing on higher-codimension transverse slices
- **step-bundle-extension** [claim](#stmt-support-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-support-extension): Local triviality of bundles, a chain of bounded modifications, properness of the chain space and the valuative criterion. -> extension of generic bundles over a finite extension of the trait
- **step-finite-slice** [claim](#stmt-support-finite-slice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-support-finite-slice): Descend support data, pick a general point and general transverse planes using the dimension bound, and compute nearby cycles on a finite neighborhood as a direct sum of stalks. -> slice with nonzero nearby cycles
- **step-full-support** [claim](#stmt-thm-full-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-support-finite-slice-2): Assume a missing component, build the compact object, extend a generic bundle, choose a slice with nonzero nearby cycles and contradict the higher-codimension vanishing. -> equality of the primed union with the whole spectral prestack
- **step-component-categories** [claim](#stmt-cor-components) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-components): Tensor the linear equivalence with the quasi-coherent sheaves of a component and use a semisimple point to show nonvanishing. -> nonzero localized categories
- **step-arithmetic-formula** [claim](#stmt-cor-arithmetic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-arithmetic): Start from the known primed trace formula and replace the primed union by the whole prestack using full support. -> canonical isomorphism with global sections on derived fixed points
- **step-parameter-adjoint** [claim](#stmt-lem-parameter-adjoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parameter-adjoint): Presentability gives the adjoint; compactness preserved by dualizability gives continuity; projection morphisms checked on compact generators. -> coherent linear structure on the right adjoint with nonzero value
- **step-eigenobjects** [claim](#stmt-cor-eigenobjects) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-eigenobjects): Compose the adjoint of evaluation at the parameter with the embedding and the inverse Langlands equivalence, and read off the Hecke eigenstructure from linearity. -> nonzero Hecke eigenobject
- **step-coefficient-sheaves** [claim](#stmt-lem-coeff-sheaves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coeff-sheaves): Constructible coefficient extension, detection of the support condition through local acyclicity, bar resolutions and smooth descent. -> scalar extension equivalences
- **step-coefficient-spectral** [claim](#stmt-lem-coeff-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coeff-spectral): Compare affine families using the sheaf comparison and pass to limits using dualizability. -> identification of spectral prestacks and quasi-coherent categories
- **step-coefficient-action** [claim](#stmt-lem-coeff-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coeff-action): Compare geometric Satake and the Hecke system under coefficient extension and invoke the equivalence of spaces of actions. -> scalar extension of the rational action is the geometric action
- **step-clopen-detection** [claim](#stmt-lem-coeff-idempotent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coeff-idempotent): Extend the idempotent, apply it to the unit object via full support, and descend vanishing by faithful flatness. -> no nonempty clopen support acts by zero
- **step-rational-support** [claim](#stmt-thm-rational-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coeff-idempotent-2): Apply the idempotent detection away from the projective line and connectedness of the spectral prestack for the projective line. -> rational spectral support
- **step-rational-unpriming** [claim](#stmt-cor-rational-arithmetic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-rational-arithmetic): Substitute the equality of the support union with the whole prestack into the given primed isomorphism. -> unprimed formula over the rational field

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-full-support**: Under Hypothesis 2 or Hypothesis 3, the primed open-and-closed union of components equals the whole restricted spectral prestack, so the restricted Langlands functor is a linear equivalence onto the full nilpotent ind-coherent category.
- **inv-component-nonvanishing**: Every connected component of the spectral stack carries a nonzero localized automorphic category equivalent to the nilpotent ind-coherent category on that component.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-weyl-order-divisible**: Apply the full-support equivalence over an arbitrary algebraically closed field when the characteristic divides the Weyl-group order or is not very good for the group. -> The arbitrary-field regime is not covered; the result is only proved over the algebraic closure of a finite field or under the stated additional restrictions.
- **anti-rational-unpriming**: Claim an unconditional arithmetic trace formula over the rational coefficient field from the rational support theorem. -> The rational primed comparison map is not constructed, so the formula is only available conditionally on that input.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existential equivalences of dg categories of sheaves on stacks of bundles and local systems, proved by specialization, Hecke transversality and slicing; no finite constructive procedure with explicit inputs and outputs is stated, so no runnable reference implementation or finite oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves categorical equivalences of dg-categories and gives no finite procedure, so there is no routine to turn into a library or program. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are derived stacks and sheaf categories with no state space, dynamics or discretisation that a simulation could step. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No explicit calculation or checker is described. The results assert existence of equivalences and nonzero objects, so no tool could be built from them. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is by contradiction through nearby-cycle vanishing and transverse slicing. Each step is existential and none is an effective procedure with a cost model. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Derived prestacks and ind-coherent categories are not finite representable data, so they give no concrete layout, index or container. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no numerical kernel, dataflow or arithmetic workload in the paper to map to hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Characteristic hypotheses such as very good primes constrain a Langlands correspondence, not security parameters. No hardness assumption or protocol appears. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Coefficients are the l-adic field and sheaf categories are algebraic. There is no learning problem, loss, data or generalisation statement. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or extremal problem is posed. The slice dimension bounds serve only to run a contradiction. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Frobenius fixed points and Weil structures are arithmetic notions. They carry no communication, consensus or fault model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only plausible downstream use is as a formal-verification target in geometric representation theory. That would need a large dg-category foundation, and the paper supplies only prose proofs, so no engineering contract follows. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-regime-b-weyl-order** (hypothesis): The arbitrary-field regime requires that the characteristic is very good for the group and does not divide the Weyl-group order. Outside this condition and outside finite-field descent, the full-support equivalence is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Applying full support over an arbitrary algebraically closed field when the characteristic divides the Weyl-group order or is not very good.', 'Treating the finite-field proof as covering all algebraically closed fields.']
- **limit-common-lie-conditions** (hypothesis): All results use the four common characteristic conditions on the group: an invariant form nondegenerate on Levi centers, Chevalley restriction for Levis, semisimple centralizers that are Levis, and nilpotents in parabolic nilradicals. Without them the Levi reduction, the central cocharacter and the half-dimensionality bound are not available. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem for a group in a characteristic where an invariant form degenerates on a Levi center.', 'Dropping the nilpotent-in-nilradical condition when bounding the nilpotent cone.']
- **limit-finite-field-descent** (hypothesis): Regime A requires the curve and the split group to descend to a finite field. The arithmetic trace formula is stated only under this regime. Blocked use: ['Stating the arithmetic formula for a curve without finite-field descent.', 'Using Frobenius fixed points when no finite-field model is fixed.']
- **limit-rational-conditional** (formal_scope): The rational unpriming statement is conditional on a given canonical primed trace isomorphism over the rational coefficient field. That comparison map is not constructed in the paper, and no rational extension of regime B is asserted. Blocked use: ['Claiming an unconditional rational arithmetic formula.', 'Extending rational support to the arbitrary-field regime.']
- **limit-rational-action-datum** (hypothesis): Rational spectral support concerns a given rational spectral-action datum; the paper does not construct a normalized rational Langlands equivalence. Blocked use: ['Reading the rational theorem as constructing a rational Langlands functor.']
- **limit-nonconstructive-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The full-support proof is by contradiction: a missing component gives a compact object chosen among projections of generators, and slices are chosen generically. No explicit object, slice or procedure is produced. Blocked use: ['Reading the slice construction as an algorithm producing explicit equations.', 'Quoting running times for the argument.']
- **limit-eigenobject-category** (edge_case): Hecke eigenobjects live in the ind-constructible nilpotent category; the paper asserts no boundedness, perversity or Weil structure. Blocked use: ['Assuming the eigenobject is perverse or bounded constructible.', 'Assuming it carries a Frobenius structure.']
- **limit-quasi-lisse-projective-line** (edge_case): The quasi-lisse and ind-lisse categories are not identified in general; the identification fails for the projective line, so the comparison of actions is stated only for curves other than the projective line. Blocked use: ['Identifying the two lisse categories on the projective line.', 'Applying the action comparison lemma to the projective line.']
- **limit-rational-coefficients-quadric** (approximation): The quadric comparison is proved with rational coefficients and need not hold with torsion coefficients of residue characteristic two. Blocked use: ['Using the comparison triangle with integral or torsion coefficients.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the $`\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell`$-linear restricted geometric Langlands equivalence for smooth projective connected curves and connected reductive groups in characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; 0, with <i>ℓ</i> ≠ <i>p</i>, in two regimes. Over $`\overline{\mathbb F}_q`$, we assume the four characteristic conditions of the restricted theory stated below. Over arbitrary algebraically closed fields, we assume these conditions and additionally that <i>p</i> is very good and does not divide the Weyl-group order. This proves Gaitsgory–Raskin's full-support conjecture [[12, Conjecture 1.3.10]](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02237v1) in these regimes.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Restricted geometric Langlands theory compares automorphic sheaves with sheaves on a stack of local systems whose variation is restricted in the sense of Arinkin–Gaitsgory–Kazhdan–Raskin–Rozenblyum–Varshavsky (Arinkin et al. 2022b) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. In positive characteristic, Gaitsgory–Raskin construct an equivalence onto an open-and-closed union of spectral components (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a, Main Theorem 1.3.9(i)) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)]. The remaining support question asks whether any component can be absent. We prove that none is absent in the two precise settings below.

The geometric comparison grew from the construction of individual Hecke eigensheaves. Over a finite field, unramified automorphic functions can be viewed as functions on isomorphism classes of bundles; Frobenius traces turn sheaves on the bundle stack into such functions. For general linear groups, the constructions of Drinfeld and Laumon led to the question of attaching a Hecke eigensheaf to each irreducible local system. Frenkel–Gaitsgory–Vilonen reduced this existence problem to a vanishing conjecture, which they established over finite fields using Lafforgue’s work (Frenkel, Gaitsgory, and Vilonen 2001, sec. 0.1 and §§1–2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[FrenkelGaitsgoryVilonen](#ref-FrenkelGaitsgoryVilonen)]. Gaitsgory subsequently proved the vanishing conjecture geometrically (Dennis Gaitsgory 2004, sec. 0.1 and §2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GaitsgoryVanishing](#ref-GaitsgoryVanishing)]. The categorical formulation of Beilinson–Drinfeld seeks a comparison of the whole automorphic category with a category varying over the stack of local systems (Arinkin et al. 2022b, sec. 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)].

The choice of spectral category is essential. In characteristic zero, Arinkin–Gaitsgory explained why quasi-coherent sheaves on the local-system stack do not suffice for a general reductive group, and formulated the comparison using ind-coherent sheaves with nilpotent singular support (Arinkin and Gaitsgory 2015, secs. 1.1.1–1.1.6) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ArinkinGaitsgory](#ref-ArinkinGaitsgory)]. The extra singular direction is a nilpotent horizontal section of the adjoint local system, reflecting the additional datum in an Arthur parameter. Their formulation also satisfies the compatibility tests supplied by geometric Satake and Eisenstein series. On the automorphic side, Beilinson’s theory supplies singular support for constructible sheaves in arbitrary characteristic (Beilinson 2017, sec. 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BeilinsonSingularSupport](#ref-BeilinsonSingularSupport)]. These two support theories concern different categories and play different roles in the correspondence.

To formulate a categorical comparison for $\ell$-adic sheaves, Arinkin–Gaitsgory–Kazhdan–Raskin–Rozenblyum–Varshavsky introduced the restricted local-system stack (Arinkin et al. 2022b, secs. 0.1–0.2 and §0.5.4) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. Its families are defined through the tensor category of local systems in the chosen sheaf theory, rather than through a de Rham moduli problem. The connected components are indexed by semisimple local systems, while each component retains extensions and derived deformation data (Arinkin et al. 2022b, Proposition 3.7.2 and Corollary 3.7.4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. They constructed the coherent spectral action on automorphic sheaves with nilpotent singular support and proved, under their characteristic hypotheses, that Hecke eigensheaves have nilpotent singular support, as predicted by Laumon (Arinkin et al. 2022b, sec. 0.2.3 and Theorems 14.3.2 and 14.4.3) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. This supplies both the categories and the Hecke-compatible action used in the present paper.

The characteristic-zero geometric Langlands theorem was established in the five-part project of Arinkin, Beraldo, Campbell, Chen, Faergeman, Gaitsgory, Lin, Raskin, and Rozenblyum. Gaitsgory–Raskin’s construction of the functor and their multiplicity-one theorem are the first and final parts of that project (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025b, 2026) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GLCI](#ref-GLCI), [GLCV](#ref-GLCV)]. Their subsequent work (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)] uses geometric specialization to relate the characteristic-zero and positive-characteristic $\ell$-adic theories. It proves the primed equivalence used here and the full result for general linear groups. Our input from characteristic zero is precisely (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a, Main Theorem 1.3.9(ii)) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)].

An equivalence onto a union of components does not yet give an automorphic category at a parameter outside that union. The issue is therefore existence on the omitted components, even after the categorical comparison has been constructed. Our proof addresses this issue by showing that the geometric specialization functor cannot annihilate the nonzero spectral summand attached to such a component. The local tests that establish this fact are described after the main statements.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The spectral stack and the two hypothesis regimes

Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$, let $\ell\ne p$ be a prime, and set $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$. Let $X/k$ be a smooth projective connected curve and $G/k$ a connected reductive group. Write $\check G/E$ for the Langlands dual group and $\mathfrak g=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G)$. Such a group $G$ is split over $k$.

<a id="intro:restricted-stack"></a>
We use the $E$-linear sheaf categories of (Arinkin et al. 2022b; Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV), [GR](#ref-GR)]. In particular, $\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}(X)$ is the ind-constructible category, and $\mathop{\mathrm{QLisse}}_E(X)$ denotes the full dg subcategory of $\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}(X)$ whose ordinary cohomology sheaves are filtered colimits of finite-rank lisse $E$-sheaves (Arinkin et al. 2022b, sec. 1.2.5 and Definition 1.2.6) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. Its distinction from the ind-completion $\mathop{\mathrm{IndLisse}}_E(X)$ of constructible lisse complexes will matter in Section <a href="#sec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:specialization">2</a>. The restricted spectral prestack $Y=LS^{\mathrm{restr}}_{\check G}(X)$ is defined on derived affine $E$-schemes $T$ by $$\label{intro:restricted-stack}
 Y(T)=\mathop{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\otimes,\mathrm{r.t.ex}}
 \bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}_E(\check G),\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(T)\otimes_E\mathop{\mathrm{QLisse}}_E(X)\bigr).$$ Here the superscript denotes right-t-exact symmetric monoidal functors, with the conventions of (Arinkin et al. 2022b) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. A parameter is an $E$-point of $Y$, hence such a tensor functor with $T=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}E$.

Write $\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(X)$ for the stack of principal $G$-bundles on $X$. Its global nilpotent cone consists of cotangent Higgs fields with nilpotent values. It defines the full dg subcategory $\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(X))$. On $Y$ we use the spectral nilpotent singular-support condition and the category $\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(Y)$ of (Arinkin et al. 2022b, secs. 21.2.1–21.2.5) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. These are the established categories; the new assertion concerns the spectral components on which the equivalence is supported.

We state the characteristic assumptions individually. A Levi subgroup always means a Levi factor of a parabolic subgroup. Semisimple Lie elements are geometrically conjugate into the Lie algebra of a maximal torus; nilpotent elements have zero in their geometric adjoint-orbit closure.

<a id="stmt-hyp-common"></a>
<div id="hyp:common" class="hypothesis">

**Hypothesis 1** (Common characteristic assumptions). The following conditions hold for $G/k$.

1.  There is a nondegenerate $G$-invariant symmetric bilinear form $\kappa$ on $\mathfrak g$ whose restriction to the center of $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(M)$ is nondegenerate for every Levi subgroup $M$.

2.  For every Levi subgroup $M$, including $G$, and a maximal torus $T_M\subset M$, Chevalley restriction is an isomorphism $$k[\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(M)]^M\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
     k[\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(T_M)]^{W_M},\qquad W_M=N_M(T_M)/T_M.$$

3.  The scheme-theoretic centralizer in $G$ of every semisimple element of $\mathfrak g$ is a Levi subgroup.

4.  For every field extension $k'/k$, every nilpotent element of $\mathfrak g\otimes_k k'$ belongs to $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(R_u(P))$ for some parabolic subgroup $P\subset G_{k'}$ defined over $k'$.

</div>

The first three conditions are those of (Arinkin et al. 2022b, sec. 14.4.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]; the last is the additional condition of (Arinkin et al. 2022b, sec. D.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. These are the assumptions referenced by (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a, sec. 0.1.9) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)]. The center in condition (i) is the center of a Lie algebra. We will justify its relation to central cocharacters where that relation is used.

<a id="stmt-hyp-a"></a>
<div id="hyp:A" class="hypothesis">

**Hypothesis 2** (Regime A: finite-field descent). There are $q=p^r$, a smooth projective geometrically connected curve $X_0/\mathbb F_q$, and a split connected reductive group $G_0/\mathbb F_q$ such that $$k=\overline{\mathbb F}_q,\qquad
 X=X_0\times_{\mathbb F_q}k,\qquad
 G=G_0\times_{\mathbb F_q}k.$$ Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a> holds. No additional restriction on the order of the Weyl group is imposed in this regime.

</div>

<a id="stmt-hyp-b"></a>
<div id="hyp:B" class="hypothesis">

**Hypothesis 3** (Regime B: arbitrary algebraically closed base). The field $k$ is any algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$. Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a> holds, $p$ is very good for $G$, and $p\nmid |W_G|$. No finite-field descent of $X$ is assumed.

</div>

Here very good has its usual root-system meaning: for type $A_n$, $p\nmid n+1$; for $B_n,C_n,D_n$, $p\ne2$; for $G_2,F_4,E_6,E_7$, $p\ne2,3$; and for $E_8$, $p\ne2,3,5$, on every irreducible factor. A torus has no such exclusions. We retain all the conditions in Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:B" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:B">3</a>, even where some are redundant for a particular step. The two regimes have distinct proofs of the needed Lie-theoretic statement; neither is used as a substitute for the other.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Full support

Under Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a>, the restricted Langlands functor of (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)] factors as <a id="intro:primed"></a>$$\label{intro:primed}
 \mathbb L_G^{\mathrm{restr}}:
 \mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(X))
 \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(Y')
 \hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(Y),$$ where $Y'\subset Y$ is an open-and-closed union of connected components. The functor is $\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y)$-linear (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a, Lemma 1.3.7 and Main Theorem 1.3.9) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)].

<a id="stmt-thm-full-support"></a>
<div id="thm:full-support" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Full support). *Let $k,\ell,E,X,G$ be as above. Under either Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:A" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:A">2</a> or Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:B" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:B">3</a>, the inclusion $Y'\subset Y$ is equality as spectral prestacks. Consequently the given functor is a $\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y)$-linear equivalence $$\mathbb L_G^{\mathrm{restr}}:
 \mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(X))\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
 \mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(Y).$$*

</div>

This proves Conjecture 1.3.10 of (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)] in the two displayed regimes. The positive-characteristic primed equivalence, the full characteristic-zero equivalence, and the full result for general linear groups are established inputs from Main Theorem 1.3.9 of that paper. The present theorem concerns the restricted theory with its stated characteristic hypotheses.

Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">8</a> records three consequences. Localizing the given functor at any spectral component gives the corresponding nonzero ind-coherent category. In regime A, the known primed trace formula becomes the full arithmetic formula using derived geometric Frobenius fixed points. For an arbitrary parameter, spectral linearity produces a nonzero Hecke eigenobject with coherent tensor and fusion compatibilities. Its asserted category is ind-constructible and nilpotent; no bounded constructibility or perversity assertion is needed.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Rational coefficients

There is also a coefficient form of the support question. Put $E_0=\mathbb Q_\ell$, retaining $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$. For the rational support statement let $k=\overline{\mathbb F}_q$, and let $X/k$ be smooth projective connected and $G/k$ connected reductive satisfying Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:common" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:common">1</a>. Let $\check G_0/E_0$ be the split dual group, with $\check G=\check G_0\times_{E_0}E$. Define $Y_0$ by <a href="#intro:restricted-stack" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:restricted-stack">[intro:restricted-stack]</a> with $E_0$ in place of $E$, and put $\mathcal C_0=\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}},E_0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G)$, using the rational ind-constructible sheaf category and the same nilpotent condition. These coefficient changes leave the geometric field $k$ unchanged.

Universal evaluation sends a representation to its local system in the universal family on $Y_0$; for a finite set of legs it uses the exterior products of these local systems. The rational spectral-action datum consists of a continuous $\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y_0)$-action on $\mathcal C_0$ whose restriction along universal evaluation is the usual multi-leg Hecke system, with its tensor, permutation and collision compatibilities. We take this datum as part of the rational support problem. The rational geometric Satake and derived Hecke constructions are recalled in Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">9</a>. We distinguish the given rational action and support from a construction of a normalized rational Langlands equivalence: the cited theorem of Gaitsgory–Raskin is used over $E$.

<a id="stmt-thm-rational-support"></a>
<div id="thm:rational-support" class="theorem">

**Theorem 5** (Rational spectral support). *For the rational datum just specified, every nonempty open-and-closed substack $U\subset Y_0$ has $$\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(U)\underset{\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y_0)}\otimes\mathcal C_0\ne0.$$ Consequently, if $Y'_0\subset Y_0$ is an open-and-closed support union whose complementary structure idempotent acts by zero on $\mathcal C_0$, then $Y'_0=Y_0$ as spectral prestacks. In particular this applies to the support of any given $\mathop{\mathrm{QCoh}}(Y_0)$-linear primed equivalence.*

</div>

The conclusion concerns the entire rational spectral prestack, without identifying its components with rational points. A curve over $\overline{\mathbb F}_q$ descends to a finite extension of $\mathbb F_q$ by finite presentation; a split form of $G$ does as well. Thus Theorem <a href="#thm:full-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:full-support">4</a> in regime A supplies the geometric input without adding a Weyl-order restriction. No rational extension of regime B is asserted here.

Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">9</a> proves the required coefficient comparison on sheaf categories, affine families and the coherent spectral action. It then descends the absence of a clopen summand acting by zero. The rational arithmetic statement has a further input: a canonical primed trace formula over $E_0$ with the stated Frobenius and ind-coherent conventions. We prove unpriming conditional on that input; we do not construct the rational primed comparison map. The unconditional arithmetic formula above remains over $E$.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The specialization and slicing argument

We first outline the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:full-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:full-support">4</a> over $E$. A missing spectral component leads to a contradiction as follows. Lift $X$ to a smooth proper curve $\mathscr X$ over the Witt trait, lift $G$, and let $\mathcal B$ be the relative bundle stack. Write $K$ for an algebraic closure of the fraction field and $\Psi$ for geometric nearby cycles with monodromy forgotten. The established specialization formalism and characteristic-zero equivalence produce, from a missing component, a nonzero compact sheaf $F$ on $\mathcal B_K$ with $\Psi F=0$.

Section <a href="#sec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:specialization">2</a> strengthens this vanishing to Hecke transforms pulled back along maps whose bundle projection is smooth. The leg map may be arbitrary. This extra freedom is needed after cutting a Hecke correspondence by a hypersurface.

On a smooth chart $U\to\mathcal B$, a special-fiber cotangent vector outside the nilpotent cone gives a test function $f$. If that covector comes from a Higgs field, Sections <a href="#sec:lie" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lie">3</a> and <a href="#sec:hecke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hecke">4</a> construct a modification with a nonzero leg covector and transverse zeros on both fixed-side Hecke fibers. The two characteristic regimes enter only in constructing the Levi and central cocharacter required for this calculation.

Section <a href="#sec:twohecke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:twohecke">5</a> takes two modifications with common output and common leg. Proper pushforward and the reverse transversality isolate the stalk at the diagonal. The forward transversality then gives an actual étale local equation $$\sum_{i=1}^m u_i v_i+f=0,$$ with the sheaf pulled back from $U$. Here $m$ is the Hecke-orbit dimension and $(u_i,v_i)$ are local coordinates transverse to the chart and leg. This is the point where the two correspondences are both essential.

Section <a href="#sec:slices" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:slices">6</a> compares the projective closure of this quadratic equation with its hyperplane classes. The remaining derived sheaf is a constant line supported on $f=0$. A projective incidence construction then gives the same vanishing on higher-codimension slices whose cotangent planes avoid the nilpotent cone. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:support">7</a> uses the nilpotent dimension bound to choose such a slice through the actual support of $F$. Its support is finite over a henselian trait near the chosen point, so its nearby cycles are a finite direct sum of stalks with at least one nonzero summand. This contradicts slice vanishing.

The central-in-a-Levi modification and moving-leg calculation originate in (Arinkin et al. 2022b, secs. 20.4–20.7) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. The additional argument here is the two-correspondence transversality and slicing mechanism, including the exact local equation and derived comparison triangles. All singular-support dimension bounds used in the proof are on the special fiber; the nearby-cycle tests themselves are proved directly. The arithmetic application combines the automorphic trace comparison of (Arinkin et al. 2022a) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[AGKRRVTrace](#ref-AGKRRVTrace)] with the spectral trace calculation of Beraldo–Lin–Reeves (Beraldo, Lin, and Reeves 2024, secs. 6.4.12–6.4.13) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[BeraldoLinReeves](#ref-BeraldoLinReeves)], as assembled in (Dennis Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025a, sec. 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)]. The coefficient argument in Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">9</a> uses constructible coefficient continuity (Hemo, Richarz, and Scholbach 2023) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[HemoRicharzScholbach](#ref-HemoRicharzScholbach)] and the universal-local-acyclicity criterion of (Hansen and Scholze 2023) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[HansenScholze](#ref-HansenScholze)]; the rational spectral action and primed arithmetic map remain the given data stated above.

<a id="sec:specialization"></a>
### Specialization and a missing spectral component

We first translate a missing component into a geometric vanishing statement. The object we obtain will be compact, hence constructible on finite-type charts. More importantly, its Hecke transforms will have zero nearby cycles after any pullback whose projection to the bundle stack is smooth. The map recording the Hecke point need not be smooth. This last qualification is what permits the hypersurface tests in Sections <a href="#sec:twohecke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:twohecke">5</a> and <a href="#sec:slices" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:slices">6</a>.

Fix data satisfying Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:A" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:A">2</a> or Hypothesis <a href="#hyp:B" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:B">3</a>. Nothing in this section assumes the full-support conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:full-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:full-support">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### A trait and geometric specialization

Initially set $$R=W(k),\qquad S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R),\qquad
 K=\overline{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}.$$ We fix the algebraic closure $K$ throughout. Since $k$ is perfect, every nonzero Witt vector is a power of $p$ times a unit. Thus $R$ is a complete Noetherian discrete valuation ring with uniformizer $p$ and residue field $k$.
